| T-441 | Fix hallkeeper offline progress sync silent data loss and poison messages. | done | 5 | 0.5 | T-135, T-136 | USER-2026-06-07 | Fixed in `packages/web/src/pages/HallkeeperPage.tsx` and `packages/web/src/lib/progress-sync-queue.ts`, with tests in `packages/web/src/lib/__tests__/progress-sync-queue.test.ts`. The offline progress flush built its "server" oracle from the optimistic local `checks` state — which already reflects every queued toggle — so `opsStillNeedingReplay` classified every op as already-converged and `ackProgress`'d (deleted) it WITHOUT issuing the PATCH: on reconnect the pending badge drained to 0 while the server never received the offline edits (silent data loss, the exact failure the queue exists to prevent). Secondary defect: terminal 4xx replies were treated as retriable, so a deleted-config / revoked-access / bad-row op became a poison message that re-fired on every mount + `online` event and never drained. Fix: flush now fetches AUTHORITATIVE server progress (`GET /hallkeeper/:configId/progress`) as the oracle and bails (keeps the queue intact) if it can't read it; new pure, fully-tested helpers `partitionReplay`, `isReplayStatusTerminal`, `resolveReplayDisposition` split queued ops into already-satisfied (drop without network) vs needing one toggle, and classify each replay as ack (success or terminal-drop) vs keep (network / 5xx / 408 / 429); a `flushInFlightRef` guard stops concurrent flushes from double-flipping the non-idempotent toggle; `handleToggle` now reverts the optimistic UI on a terminal rejection instead of queueing a doomed op; the flush effect's deps narrowed to `[configId]` so it no longer re-subscribes on every checkbox toggle. Tests: progress-sync-queue +12 (18 total). Verified web typecheck, lint, full suite (97 files / 1797 tests), and production build. Residual TOCTOU between the authoritative GET and the toggle PATCH is inherent to the toggle contract — tracked by T-442. Web-only; no API/schema, public copy, or package rename changed. |
| T-442 | Make hallkeeper progress PATCH idempotent (set-state) to close the replay TOCTOU. | done | 4 | 0.5 | T-441 | USER-2026-06-07 | Fixed in `packages/api/src/routes/hallkeeper-sheet.ts`, `packages/api/src/lib/hallkeeper-progress.ts`, `packages/api/src/__tests__/{hallkeeper-progress,hallkeeper-sheet-v2}.test.ts`, `packages/web/src/pages/HallkeeperPage.tsx`, and `packages/web/e2e/hallkeeper.spec.ts`. `PATCH /hallkeeper/:configId/progress` now accepts optional `checked: boolean`: `{ rowKey, checked }` is idempotent set-state and `{ rowKey }` preserves the legacy toggle path. Checked=true uses `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on `(config_id,row_key)` so duplicate/lost/concurrent replay converges without surfacing unique-constraint 500s; checked=false deletes directly by `(config_id,row_key)` and safely no-ops if absent. Added pure mutation tests for insert/delete/noop/toggle, validation guards for `checked`, a source guard for conflict-safe insert, and Hallkeeper E2E assertion that the web client sends `checked: boolean`. Verified API typecheck, lint, focused progress/sheet tests (40), full default API test suite (54 files / 1607 tests), web typecheck, lint, progress-sync-queue tests (18), web build, API build, and Hallkeeper E2E (18 passed). API integration suite remains excluded from the default command and was not run against a live database in this pass. |

(2) Pre-existing product bug: `.venviewer-planner-shell` declares `isolation: isolate`, trapping the AuthModal overlay's zIndex 200 inside the shell stacking context while the status header (z46) and the 3D/2D view-mode pill (z31) sit outside it in the root context, so planner chrome painted above — and was clickable on top of — an open sign-in modal; `packages/web/src/components/editor/AuthModal.tsx` now portals to `document.body`. Attribution verified by stashing the T-447 strand and reproducing against clean HEAD. (3) T-447 consequence: the command deck now exposes Undo/Redo alongside the toolbar, so editor.spec's page-wide Undo role query violated Playwright strict mode; the toolbar-presence assertion is now scoped to `planner-toolbar`. Verified serialized editor suite (editor, editor-interactions, editor-mobile-shell) 38/38 green; full serialized web E2E 95 passed / 1 failed / 4 skipped in 4.9 min, the single failure being the pre-existing API-coupled trades-hall-visual console guard tracked as T-449; web typecheck, lint, and the AuthModal unit contract suite (save-send-panel, 30 tests). Without it, the validation passes are not actually runnable. ADR designs the small `vsir_qa_jobs` table (approximately 6-8 columns: id, pass_name, subject_id, subject_type, status, attempt_count, error, timestamps) and the worker dispatch protocol. | T-112 | D-021 ADR: VSIR-0 partition strategy for long-lived growth. | deferred | 2 | 0.5 | VSIR-0 implementation in production AND row count in `vsir_spatial_layer_versions` or `vsir_qa_certificates` exceeds approximately 100K rows | D-019 | All four VSIR-0 sources skipped partitioning. Acceptable for v0.1 (Trades Hall is one venue, growth is slow) but documents trigger conditions and recommended scheme: HASH or LIST partitioning by `venue_id` (group all rows per venue together), OR RANGE partitioning by `created_at` month/year (group by time window), depending on query patterns and row volume. Range partitioning directly on `venue_id` is NOT applicable — UUIDs don't have meaningful range ordering. Activate when row counts justify; until then, pure documentation. |

## Status legend

- **not-started** — proposed, not yet picked up.
- **in-progress** — actively being worked.
- **done** — completed.
- **deferred** — tracked but not committed; conditions for revisit in Notes.
- **blocked** — depends on something not yet ready.
- **rejected** — considered and rejected; kept for traceability.

## Shepherd protocol

This task list is a living document. Every Claude Code session must follow this protocol.

### Before starting work

- Read this file in full.
- Identify the T-NNN that the current request maps to. If no T-NNN matches, propose a new T-NNN in the appropriate tier and add it to the table before starting work.
- Update the matched task's status to `in-progress`.

### During work

If the work becomes blocked:

- Set status to `blocked`.
- Add to Notes: what blocks the task and what would unblock it.
- Surface the block in the session log at `docs/sessions/YYYY-MM-DD.md`.

If the work reveals new tasks (subtasks, prerequisite work, follow-on work):

- Add them to the appropriate tier with explicit `Depends` linkage.
- Do not merge new work into the in-progress task scope without Blake's explicit confirmation.

### After completing work

- Set the task's status to `done`.
- Update the Notes field with the actual delivered artifact paths (e.g. "Implementation at `infra/runpod/run_training.sh`, runbook at `infra/runpod/RUNBOOK.md`").
- Update any tasks that were `Depends`-on-this-task to surface that they're now unblocked.
- Regenerate `docs/diagrams/task-graph.md` to reflect the new state.
- Add a session log entry to `docs/sessions/YYYY-MM-DD.md` noting the completed task and any newly-unblocked tasks.

### Per-session surveillance

Each session also checks:

- Any task that's been `in-progress` for >7 days: probably stuck. Surface to Blake.
- Any task whose `Depends` are now all `done`: should be flagged as ready to start.
- Any task whose `Depends` include a task that's been `rejected`: may need re-thinking; surface to Blake.

The shepherd protocol is non-negotiable. Skipping it produces the exact failure mode this protocol was designed to prevent: tasks that quietly stay open forever because nobody updated them after the work shipped.
